The defect is executable on the exact parent, not a durability conjecture. A bounded probe patched `os.replace` to perform the real publication, unlink the published regular file and install a FIFO under the same destination name. It emitted `FIFO_INSTALLED_BEFORE_PUBLISHED_OPEN`, never reached a type refusal and was killed after one second with return code `-9`. The same blocking primitive exists at the stage and final-identity opens. The fixed code opens each name with `O_NONBLOCK`; a FIFO descriptor is then available without a writer and the existing `S_ISREG` or identity predicate refuses it. The shared flag calculation also occurs before opening a parent descriptor where a missing platform flag could otherwise leak that descriptor.

The repair does not weaken publication. Stage bytes are still written through an exclusive no-follow descriptor, flushed and `fsync`ed; the named stage must retain the descriptor identity; the parent topology is rebound; `replace` and parent `fsync` still precede a bounded byte-for-byte reread; and the final repository-routed named identity must still match. A FIFO, directory, hardlink, symlink or socket cannot be accepted as the output. The documented same-uid mutation after the final named-path check remains the terminal residual because no open-file protocol can prevent a later authorised writer from changing the path after return.

Production `_read_regular` and the independent oracle retain their separate trust paths. Both walk repository parents by no-follow directory descriptor, open the final name no-follow and nonblocking, require a one-link regular file, enforce the `fstat` size and incremental read caps, compare pre/post identity and reopen the name to bind the observed inode. The oracle imports none of production's reader, parser, manifest, profile or graph implementation and retains test-owned 8 MiB JSON and 2 MiB source limits. Its single authenticated shallow snapshot still binds 190 corpus paths, 194 digest-bound source objects and the same 2,295 sorted repository paths; later live reads can prove drift but cannot replace captured bytes or topology.

A hostile-kind matrix promptly refuses hardlinks, symlinks, FIFOs, directories and UNIX sockets in both readers. The production messages are respectively the single-link regular-file or unavailable/unsafe refusals; the independent oracle supplies its own corresponding assertions. `/dev/null` is rejected before open because it leaves the repository, and a symlink to any device is rejected no-follow. Existing concurrent-parent-swap, inventory-swap, coherent-reseal, partial-read, over-cap, shallow-source-drift and exact-topology guards remain green. No privileged in-repository device creation was attempted; the shared `S_ISREG` predicate is the same refusal reached by every opened non-regular inode.

The link preflight is O(characters) with constant auxiliary state. Its pending bit represents one or more literal `](` candidates since the last `)`; one later close clears every earlier candidate, which preserves the regex's first-close work bound without a per-candidate `find`. LF is the only regex-crossable boundary allowed to reset the existing 16,384-character segment. CR and Unicode whitespace do not reset it, so each counts toward that same cap and cannot extend candidate work beyond 16,384 characters. The frozen corpus contains zero regex matches spanning LF. The root cause was a type assumption in the shared `_require_fields` boundary. Most public file paths first pass `_decode_record`, which already rejects a non-object JSON root, and frozen verifier inputs are additionally bound by inventory identity. Direct validator calls and nested helper reuse did not share that guarantee. The repair adds one `isinstance(value, dict)` refusal before creating either field set. It does not coerce values, weaken the closed-field comparison, reorder valid records or change any caller's accepted object shape.

The guard deliberately includes the equality edge: an arbitrary list was already refused because its element set differed from the allowed keys, while a list containing exactly every required key exposed the later unsafe lookup. The nested specimen recomputes its projection digest and temporarily binds the direct validator's expected projection to reach the record boundary rather than stopping at an unrelated digest check. The assertion converts every unexpected exception or acceptance into a test failure, while the repaired path must raise the module's controlled `Refusal` containing `must be an object`.
